| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | Not finding in The circuit of my breast any gross stuff<br>To form me like your blazon holds me to<br>This gentleness of answer—’tis your passion<br>That thus mistakes, the which, to you being enemy,<br>Cannot to me be kind. [[concepts/honour\|Honour]] and honesty<br>I cherish and depend on, howsoe’er<br>You skip them in me, and with them, fair coz,<br>I’ll maintain my proceedings. Pray be pleased<br>To show in generous terms your griefs, since that<br>Your question’s with your equal, who professes<br>To clear his own way with the mind and sword<br>Of a true gentleman. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | That thou durst, [[characters/arcite\|Arcite]]! |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | My coz, my coz, you have been [[motifs/well\|well]] advertised<br>How much I dare; you’ve seen me use my sword<br>Against th’advice of [[concepts/fear\|fear]]. Sure, of another<br>You would not hear me doubted, but your [[motifs/silence\|silence]]<br>Should break out, though i’th’ sanctuary. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | Sir, I have seen you move in such a place which [[motifs/well\|well]]<br>Might justify your manhood; you were called<br>A good knight and a bold. But the whole week’s not<br>fair<br>If any day it rain: their valiant temper<br>Men lose when they incline to treachery,<br>And then they fight like compelled bears—would fly<br>Were they not tied. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | Kinsman, you might as [[motifs/well\|well]] Speak this and act it in your glass as to<br>His ear which now disdains you. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | Come up to me, Quit me of these cold gyves, give me a sword,<br>Though it be rusty, and the [[concepts/charity\|charity]]<br>Of one meal lend me. Come before me then,<br>A good sword in thy hand, and do but say<br>That Emily is thine-I will forgive<br>The trespass thou hast done me, yea, my life,<br>If then thou carry’t; and brave souls in shades<br>That have died manly, which will seek of me<br>Some news from earth, they shall get none but this—<br>That thou art brave and noble. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | Be content, Again betake you to your hawthorn house.<br>With counsel of the [[motifs/night\|night]] I will be here<br>With wholesome viands. These impediments<br>Will I file off. You shall have garments and<br>Perfumes to kill the smell o’th’ [[motifs/prison\|prison]]. After,<br>When you shall stretch yourself and say but ’[[characters/arcite\|Arcite]],<br>I am in plight’, there shall be at your choice<br>Both sword and armour. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | O, you heavens, dares any So noble bear a guilty business! None<br>But only [[characters/arcite\|Arcite]], therefore none but [[characters/arcite\|Arcite]]<br>In this kind is so bold. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | Sweet [[characters/palamon\|Palamon]]. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | I do embrace you and your offer—for<br>Your offer do’t I only, sir; your person,<br>Without hypocrisy, I may not wish |
| *(didascalia)* | [[motifs/wind\|Wind]] horns within |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | More than my sword’s edge on’t. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | You hear the horns—Enter your muset lest this match between’s<br>Be crossed ere met. Give me your hand, farewell.<br>I’ll bring you every needful thing—I pray you,<br>Take comfort and be strong. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | Pray hold your promise, And do the deed with a bent brow. Most certain<br>You [[motifs/love\|love]] me not—be rough with me and pour<br>This oil out of your language. By this air,<br>I could for each word give a cuff, my stomach<br>Not reconciled by reason. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | Plainly spoken, Yet—pardon me—hard language: when I spur |
| *(didascalia)* | [[motifs/wind\|Wind]] horns within |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | My [[motifs/horse\|horse]] I chide him not. Content and anger<br>In me have but one face. Hark, sir, they call<br>The scattered to the banquet. You must guess<br>I have an office there. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | Sir, your attendance Cannot please heaven, and I know your office<br>Unjustly is achieved. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | ’Tis a good title. I am persuaded this question, sick between’s,<br>By bleeding must be cured. I am a suitor<br>That to your sword you will bequeath this plea<br>And talk of it no more. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | But this one word: You are going now to gaze upon my mistress—<br>For note you, mine she is— |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | Nay then— |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | Nay, pray you—You talk of feeding me to breed me strength—<br>You are going now to look upon a sun<br>That strengthens what it looks on. There you have<br>A vantage o’er me, but enjoy it till<br>I may enforce my remedy. Farewell. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |
| *(didascalia)* | severally, [[characters/palamon\|Palamon]] |
| *(didascalia)* | as into the bush |

### Atto 3, Scena 2

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Jailer’s Daughter, with a file |
| JAILER’S DAUGHTER | He has mistook the brake I meant, is gone<br>After his fancy. ’Tis now wellnigh morning.<br>No matter—would it were perpetual [[motifs/night\|night]],<br>And darkness lord o’th’ world. Hark, ’tis a wolf!<br>In me hath grief slain [[concepts/fear\|fear]], and, but for one thing,<br>I care for nothing—and that’s [[characters/palamon\|Palamon]].<br>I reck not if the wolves would jaw me, so<br>He had this file. What if I hollered for him?<br>I cannot holler. If I whooped, what then?<br>If he not answered, I should call a wolf<br>And do him but that service. I have heard<br>Strange howls this livelong [[motifs/night\|night]]—why may’t not be<br>They have made prey of him? He has no weapons;<br>He cannot run; the jangling of his gyves<br>Might call fell things to listen, who have in them<br>A sense to know a man unarmed, and can<br>Smell where resistance is. I’ll set it down<br>He’s torn to pieces: they howled many together<br>And then they fed on him. So much for that.<br>Be bold to ring the bell. How stand I then?<br>All’s chared when he is gone. No, no, I lie:<br>My father’s to be hanged for his escape,<br>Myself to beg, if I prized life so much<br>As to deny my act—but that I would not,<br>Should I try [[motifs/death\|death]] by dozens. I am moped—<br>Food took I none these two days,<br>Sipped some [[motifs/water\|water]]. I have not closed mine eyes<br>Save when my lids scoured off their brine. Alas,<br>Dissolve, my life; let not my sense unsettle,<br>Lest I should drown or stab or hang myself.<br>O state of [[concepts/nature\|nature]], fail together in me,<br>Since thy best props are warped. So which way now?<br>The best way is the next way to a [[motifs/grave\|grave]],<br>Each errant step beside is torment. Lo,<br>The [[motifs/moon\|moon]] is down, the crickets chirp, the screech-owl<br>Calls in the [[motifs/dawn\|dawn]]. All offices are done<br>Save what I fail in: but the point is this,<br>An end, and that is all. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |

### Atto 3, Scena 3

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/arcite\|Arcite]] with a bundle containing meat, wine, and |
| *(didascalia)* | files |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | I should be near the place. Ho, cousin Patamon! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/palamon\|Palamon]] as from the bush |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | [[characters/arcite\|Arcite]]. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | The same. I have brought you food and files. Come forth and [[concepts/fear\|fear]] not, here’s no [[characters/theseus\|Theseus]]. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | Nor none so honest, [[characters/arcite\|Arcite]]. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | That’s no matter—We’ll argue that hereafter. Come, take [[concepts/courage\|courage]]—<br>You shall not die thus beastly. Here, sir, drink;<br>I know you are faint. Then I’ll talk further with you. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | [[characters/arcite\|Arcite]], thou mightst now [[motifs/poison\|poison]] me. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | I might—But I must [[concepts/fear\|fear]] you first. Sit down and, good now,<br>No more of these vain parleys. Let us not,<br>Having our ancient reputation with us,<br>Make talk for fools and cowards. To your health, sir. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | Do. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/arcite\|Arcite]] drinks |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | Pray sit down, then, and let me entreat you, By all the honesty and [[concepts/honour\|honour]] in you, No mention of this woman—’twilt disturb us. We shall have [[motifs/time\|time]] enough. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | [[motifs/well\|Well]], sir, I’ll pledge you.<br>[[characters/palamon\|Palamon]] drinks |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | Drink a good hearty draught; it breeds good [[motifs/blood\|blood]],<br>man.<br>Do not you feel it thaw you? |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | Stay, I’ll tell you<br>After a draught or two more.<br>[[characters/palamon\|Palamon]] drinks |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | Spare it not—<br>The Duke has more, coz. Eat now. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | Yes.<br>[[characters/palamon\|Palamon]] eats |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | I am glad<br>You have so good a stomach. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | I am gladder<br>I have so good meat to’t. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | Is’t not mad, lodging Here in the wild woods, cousin? |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | Yes, for them<br>That have wild consciences. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | How tastes your victuals?<br>Your hunger needs no sauce, I see. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | Not much.<br>But if it did, yours is too tart, sweet cousin.<br>What is this? |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | Venison. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | ’Tis a lusty meat—<br>Give me more wine. Here, [[characters/arcite\|Arcite]], to the wenches |
| *(didascalia)* | We have known in our days. Drinking The lord |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | steward’s daughter.<br>Do you remember her? |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | After you, coz. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | She loved a black-haired man. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | She did so; [[motifs/well\|well]], sir. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | And I have heard some call him [[characters/arcite\|Arcite]], and— |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | Out with’t, [[concepts/faith\|faith]]. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | She met him in an arbour—<br>What did she there, coz? Play o’th’ virginals? |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | Something she did, sir— |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | Made her groan a month for’t—<br>Or two, or three, or ten. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | The marshal’s sister Had her share too, as I remember, cousin,<br>Else there be tales abroad. You’ll pledge her? |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | Yes. |
| *(didascalia)* | They drink |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | A pretty brown wench ’tis. There was a [[motifs/time\|time]]<br>When young men went a-hunting, and a wood,<br>And a broad beech, and thereby hangs a tale—<br>Heigh-ho! |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | For Emily, upon my life! [[archetypes/fool\|Fool]],<br>Away with this strained mirth. I say again,<br>That sigh was breathed for Emily. Base cousin,<br>Dar’st thou break first? |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | You are wide. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | By heaven and earth,<br>There’s nothing in thee honest. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | Then I’ll leave you—<br>You are a beast now. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | As thou mak’st me, traitor. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | (pointing to the bundle)<br>There’s all things needful: files and shirts and<br>perfumes—<br>I’ll come again some two hours hence and bring<br>That that shall quiet all. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | A sword and armour. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | [[concepts/fear\|Fear]] me not. You are now too foul. Farewell.<br>Get off your trinkets: you shall want naught. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | Sirrah— |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | I’ll hear no more. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | If he keep touch, he dies for’t. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit as into the bush |

### Atto 3, Scena 4

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Jailer’s Daughter |
| JAILER’S DAUGHTER | I am very cold, and all the [[motifs/stars\|stars]] are out too,<br>The little [[motifs/stars\|stars]] and all, that look like agtets—<br>The sun has seen my folly. [[characters/palamon\|Palamon]]!<br>Alas, no, he’s in heaven. Where am I now?<br>Yonder’s the sea and there’s a [[motifs/ship\|ship]]—how’t tumbles!<br>And there’s a rock lies watching under [[motifs/water\|water]]—<br>Now, now, it beats upon it—now, now, now,<br>There’s a leak sprung, a sound one—how they cry!<br>Open her before the [[motifs/wind\|wind]]—you’ll lose all else.<br>Up with a course or two and tack about, boys.<br>Good [[motifs/night\|night]], good [[motifs/night\|night]], you’re gone. I am very<br>hungry.<br>Would I could find a fine frog—he would tell me<br>News from all parts o‘th’ world, then would I make<br>A carrack of a cockle-shell, and sail<br>By east and north-east to the [[archetypes/king\|King]] of Pygmies,<br>For he tells fortunes rarely. Now my father,<br>Twenty to one, is trussed up in a trice<br>Tomorrow morning. I’ll say never a word.<br>(She sings)<br>For I’ll cut my green coat, a foot above my knee,<br>And I’ll clip my yellow locks, an inch below mine eye,<br>Hey nonny, nonny, nonny,<br>He s’buy me a white cut, forth for to ride,<br>And I’ll go seek him, through the world that is so wide,<br>Hey nonny, nonny, nonny.<br>O for a prick now, like a [[motifs/nightingale\|nightingale]],<br>To put my breast against. I shall [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]] like a top else. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |

### Atto 3, Scena 5

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Gerald (a schoolmaster), five Countrymen, one of whom is dressed as a Babion, five Wenches, and Timothy, a taborer. All are attired as morris dancers |
| SCHOOLMASTER | Fie, fie,<br>What tediosity and disinsanity<br>Is here among yet Have my rudiments<br>Been laboured so long with ye, milked unto ye,<br>And, by a figure, even the very plum-broth<br>And marrow of my understanding laid upon ye?<br>And do you still cry ‘where?’ and ‘how?’ and<br>‘wherefore?’<br>You most coarse frieze capacities, ye jean judgements,<br>Have I said, ‘thus let be’, and ‘there let be’,<br>And ‘then let be’, and no man understand me?<br>Proh deum, medius fidius-ye are all dunces.<br>Forwhy, here stand I. Here the Duke comes. There are<br>you,<br>Close in the thicket. The Duke appears. I meet him,<br>And unto him I utter learnèd things<br>And many figures. He hears, and nods, and hums,<br>And then cries, ‘Rare!’, and I go forward. At length<br>I fling my cap up—mark there—then do you,<br>As once did Meleager and the boar,<br>Break comely out before him, like true lovers,<br>Cast yourselves in a [[concepts/body\|body]] decently,<br>And sweetly, by a figure, trace and turn, boys. |
| FIRST COUNTRYMAN | And sweetly we will do it, master Gerald. |
| SECOND COUNTRYMAN | Draw up the company. Where’s the taborer? |
| THIRD COUNTRYMAN | Why, Timothy! |
| TABORER | Here, my mad boys, have at ye! |
| SCHOOLMASTER | But I say, where’s these women? |
| FOURTH COUNTRYMAN | Here’s Friz and [[characters/madeline\|Madeline]]. |
| SECOND COUNTRYMAN | And little Luce with the white legs, and bouncing Barbara. |
| FIRST COUNTRYMAN | And freckled Nell, that never failed her master. |
| SCHOOLMASTER | Where be your ribbons, maids? Swim with your bodies<br>And carry it sweetly and deliverly,<br>And now and then a favour and a frisk. |
| NELL | Let us alone, sir. |
| SCHOOLMASTER | Where’s the rest o’th’ music? |
| THIRD COUNTRYMAN | Dispersed as you commanded. |
| SCHOOLMASTER | Couple, then,<br>And see what’s wanting. Where’s the babion?<br>(To the Babion) My friend, carry your tail without<br>offence<br>Or scandal to the ladies; and be sure<br>You tumble with audacity and manhood,<br>And when you bark, do it with judgement. |
| BABION | Yes, sir. |
| SCHOOLMASTER | Quousque tandem? Here is a woman wanting! |
| FOURTH COUNTRYMAN | We may go whistle—all the fat’s i’th’ [[motifs/fire\|fire]]. |
| SCHOOLMASTER | We have,<br>As learned authors utter, washed a tile;<br>We have been fatuus, and laboured vainly. |
| SECOND COUNTRYMAN | This is that scornful piece, that scurvy hilding<br>That gave her promise faithfully she would be here—<br>Cicely, the seamstress’ daughter.<br>The next gloves that I give her shall be dogskin.<br>Nay, an she fail me once—you can tell, Areas,<br>She swore by wine and bread she would not break. |
| SCHOOLMASTER | An eel and woman,<br>A learned poet says, unless by th’ tail<br>And with thy teeth thou hold, will either fail—<br>In manners this was false position. |
| FIRST COUNTRYMAN | A [[motifs/fire\|fire]]-ill take her! Does she flinch now? |
| THIRD COUNTRYMAN | What<br>Shall we determine, sir? |
| SCHOOLMASTER | Nothing;<br>Our business is become a nullity,<br>Yea, and a woeful and a piteous nullity. |
| FOURTH COUNTRYMAN | Now, when the credit of our town lay on it,<br>Now to be frampold, now to piss o’th’ nettle!<br>Go thy ways—I’ll remember thee, I’ll fit thee! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Jailer’s Daughter |
| JAILER’S DAUGHTER | (sings)<br>The George Alow came from the south,<br>From the coast of Barbary-a;<br>And there he met with brave gallants of war,<br>By one, by two, by three-a.<br>‘[[motifs/well\|Well]] hailed, [[motifs/well\|well]] hailed, you jolly gallants,<br>And whither now are you bound-a?<br>O let me have your company<br>Till I come to the sound-a.’<br>There was three fools fell out about an owlet—<br>The one he said it was an owl,<br>The other he said nay,<br>The third he said it was a hawk,<br>And her [[motifs/bells\|bells]] were cut away. |
| THIRD COUNTRYMAN | There’s a dainty [[archetypes/madwoman\|madwoman]], master,<br>Comes i’th’ nick, as mad as a March hare.<br>If we can get her [[motifs/dance\|dance]], we are made again.<br>I warrant her, she’ll do the rarest gambols. |
| FIRST COUNTRYMAN | A [[archetypes/madwoman\|madwoman]]? We are made, boys. |
| SCHOOLMASTER | (to the Jailer’s Daughter)<br>And are you mad, good woman? |
| JAILER’S DAUGHTER | I would be sorry else.<br>Give me your hand. |
| SCHOOLMASTER | Why? |
| JAILER’S DAUGHTER | I can tell your fortune. |
| *(didascalia)* | She examines his hand |
| JAILER’S DAUGHTER | You are a [[archetypes/fool\|fool]]. Tell ten—I have posed him. Buzz!<br>Friend, you must eat no white bread—if you do,<br>Your teeth will bleed extremely. Shall we [[motifs/dance\|dance]], ho?<br>I know you—you’re a tinker. Sirrah tinker,<br>Stop no more holes but what you should. |
| SCHOOLMASTER | Dii boni—<br>A tinker, [[archetypes/damsel\|damsel]]? |
| JAILER’S DAUGHTER | Or a conjurer—<br>Raise me a devil now and let him play<br>Qui passa o’th’ [[motifs/bells\|bells]] and bones. |
| SCHOOLMASTER | Go, take her,<br>And fluently persuade her to a peace.<br>Et opus exegi, quod nec Iovis ira, nec ignis—<br><br>Strike up, and lead her in. |
| SECOND COUNTRYMAN | Come, lass, let’s trip it. |
| JAILER’S DAUGHTER | I’ll lead. |
| THIRD COUNTRYMAN | Do, do. |
| SCHOOLMASTER | Persuasively and cunningly— |
| *(didascalia)* | [[motifs/wind\|Wind]] horns within |
| SCHOOLMASTER | away, boys,<br>I hear the horns. Give me some meditation,<br>And mark your cue. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt all but Gerald the Schoolmaster Pallas inspire me. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/theseus\|Theseus]], Pirithous, [[characters/hippolyta\|Hippolyta]], [[characters/emilia\|Emilia]], [[characters/arcite\|Arcite]], and train |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | This way the stag took. |
| SCHOOLMASTER | Stay and edify. |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | What have we here? |
| PIRITHOUS | Some country sport, upon my life, sir. |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | (to the Schoolmaster)<br>[[motifs/well\|Well]], sir, go forward—we will edify.<br>Ladies, sit down—we’ll stay it. |
| *(didascalia)* | They sit: [[characters/theseus\|theseus]] in a chair, the others on stools |
| SCHOOLMASTER | Thou doughty Duke, all hail! All hail, sweet ladies. |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | This is a cold beginning. |
| SCHOOLMASTER | If you but favour, our country pastime made is.<br>We are a few of those collected here,<br>That ruder tongues distinguish ‘villager’;<br>And to say verity, and not to [[forms/fable\|fable]],<br>We are a merry rout, or else a rabble,<br>Or company, or, by a figure, [[characters/chorus\|chorus]],<br>That fore thy dignity will [[motifs/dance\|dance]] a morris.<br>And I, that am the rectifier of all,<br>By title pedagogus, that let fall<br>The birch upon the breeches of the small ones,<br>And humble with a ferula the tall ones,<br>Do here present this [[motifs/machine\|machine]], or this frame;<br>And dainty Duke, whose doughty dismal fame<br>From Dis to Daedalus, from post to pillar,<br>Is blown abroad, help me, thy poor [[motifs/well\|well]]-willer,<br>And with thy twinkling eyes, look right and straight<br>Upon this mighty ‘Moor’—of mickle weight—<br>‘Ice’ now comes in, which, being glued together,<br>Makes ‘morris’, and the cause that we came hither.<br>The [[concepts/body\|body]] of our sport, of no small study,<br>I first appear, though rude, and raw, and muddy,<br>To speak, before thy noble grace, this tenor<br>At whose great feet I offer up my penner.<br>The next, the Lord of May and Lady bright;<br>The Chambermaid and Servingman, by [[motifs/night\|night]]<br>That seek out silent hanging; then mine Host<br>And his fat Spouse, that welcomes, to their cost,<br>The galled traveller, and with a beck’ning<br>Informs the tapster to inflame the reck‘ning;<br>Then the beest-eating Clown; and next, the [[archetypes/fool\|Fool]];<br>The babion with long tail and eke long tool,<br>Cum multis aliis that make a [[motifs/dance\|dance]]—<br>Say ‘ay’, and all shall presently advance. |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | Ay, ay, by any means, dear dominie. |
| PIRITHOUS | Produce. |
| SCHOOLMASTER | (knocks for the [[motifs/dance\|dance]])<br>Intrate filii, come forth and foot it. |
| *(didascalia)* | He flings up his cap. Music. |
| *(didascalia)* | The Schoolmaster ushers in |
| SCHOOLMASTER | May Lord,<br>Servingman,<br>A Country Clown,<br>or Shepherd,<br>An Host,<br>A He-babion,<br>May Lady.<br><br>Chambermaid.<br>Country Wench.<br><br>Hostess.<br>She-babion.<br><br>A He-[[archetypes/fool\|fool]], The Jailer’s Daughter as<br><br>She-[[archetypes/fool\|fool]].<br>All these persons apparelled to the life, the men<br>issuing out of one door and the wenches from the |
| *(didascalia)* | other. They [[motifs/dance\|dance]] a morris |
| SCHOOLMASTER | Ladies, if we have been merry,<br>And have pleased ye with a derry,<br>And a derry, and a down,<br>Say the schoolmaster’s no clown.<br>Duke, if we have pleased thee too,<br>And have done as good boys should do,<br>Give us but a tree or twain<br>For a maypole, and again,<br>Ere another year run out,<br>We’ll make thee laugh, and all this rout. |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | Take twenty, dominie. (To [[characters/hippolyta\|Hippolyta]]) How does my sweetheart? |
| [[characters/hippolyta\|HIPPOLYTA]] | Never so pleased, sir. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | ’Twas an excellent [[motifs/dance\|dance]],<br>And for a preface, I never heard a better. |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | Schoolmaster, I thank you. One see ’em all rewarded. |
| PIRITHOUS | And here’s something to paint your pole withal.<br>He gives them money |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | Now to our sports again. |
| SCHOOLMASTER | May the stag thou hunt’st stand long,<br>And thy dogs be swift and strong;<br>May they kill him without lets,<br>And the ladies eat his dowsets. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[characters/theseus\|Theseus]] and train. [[motifs/wind\|Wind]] horns within |
| SCHOOLMASTER | Come, we are all made. Dii deaeque omnes,<br>Ye have danced rarely, wenches. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 3, Scena 6

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/palamon\|Palamon]] from the bush |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | About this hour my cousin gave his [[concepts/faith\|faith]]<br>To visit me again, and with him bring<br>Two swords and two good armours; if he fail,<br>He’s neither man nor soldier. When he left me,<br>I did not think a week could have restored<br>My lost strength to me, I was grown so low<br>And crest-fall’n with my wants. I thank thee, [[characters/arcite\|Arcite]],<br>Thou art yet a fair foe, and I feel myself,<br>With this refreshing, able once again<br>To out-dure danger. To delay it longer<br>Would make the world think, when it comes to<br>hearing,<br>That I lay fatting, like a swine, to fight,<br>And not a soldier. Therefore this blest morning<br>Shall be the last; and that sword he refuses,<br>If it but hold, I kill him with; ’tis justice.<br>So, [[motifs/love\|love]] and fortune for me! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/arcite\|Arcite]] with two armours and two swords |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | O, good morrow. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | Good morrow, noble kinsman. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | I have put you<br>To too much pains, sir. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | That too much, fair cousin,<br>Is but a debt to [[concepts/honour\|honour]], and my duty. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | Would you were so in all, sir—I could wish ye<br>As kind a kinsman, as you force me find<br>A beneficial foe, that my embraces<br>Might thank ye, not my blows. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | I shall think either,<br>[[motifs/well\|Well]] done, a noble recompense. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | Then I shall quit you. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | Defy me in these fair terms, and you show<br>More than a mistress to me—no more anger,<br>As you [[motifs/love\|love]] anything that’s honourable.<br>We were not bred to talk, man. When we are armed<br>And both upon our guards, then let our fury,<br>Like meeting of two tides, fly strongly from us;<br>And then to whom the birthright of this [[concepts/beauty\|beauty]]<br>Truly pertains—without upbraidings, scorns,<br>Despisings of our persons, and such poutings<br>Fitter for girls and schoolboys—will be seen,<br>And quickly, yours or mine. Will’t please you arm,<br>sir?<br>Or, if you feel yourself not fitting yet,<br>And furnished with your old strength, I’ll stay,<br>cousin,<br>And every day discourse you into health,<br>As I am spared. Your person I am friends with,<br>And I could wish I had not said I loved her,<br>Though I had died; but loving such a lady,<br>And justifying my [[motifs/love\|love]], I must not fly from’t. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | [[characters/arcite\|Arcite]], thou art so brave an enemy<br>That no man but thy cousin’s fit to kill thee.<br>I am [[motifs/well\|well]] and lusty—choose your arms. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | Choose you, sir. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | Wilt thou exceed in all, or dost thou do it<br>To make me spare thee? |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | If you think so, cousin,<br>You are deceived, for as I am a soldier,<br>I will not spare you. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | That’s [[motifs/well\|well]] said. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | You’ll find it. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | Then as I am an honest man, and [[motifs/love\|love]]<br>With all the justice of affection,<br>I’ll pay thee soundly.<br>He chooses one armour<br>This I’ll take. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | (indicating the remaining armour)<br>That’s mine, then.<br>I’ll arm you first. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | Do.<br>[[characters/arcite\|Arcite]] arms [[characters/palamon\|Palamon]]<br>Pray thee tell me, cousin,<br>Where gott’st thou this good armour? |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | ‘Tis the Duke’s,<br>And to say true, I stole it. Do I pinch you? |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | No. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | Is’t not too heavy? |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | I have worn a lighter—<br>But I shall make it serve. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | I’ll buckle’t close. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | By any means. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | You care not for a grand guard? |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | No, no, we’ll use no horses. I perceive<br>You would fain be at that fight. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | I am indifferent. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | [[concepts/faith\|Faith]], so am I. Good cousin, thrust the buckle<br>Through far enough. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | I warrant you. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | My casque now. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | Will you fight bare-armed? |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | We shall be the nimbler. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | But use your gauntlets, though—those are o’th’ least.<br>Prithee take mine, good cousin. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | Thank you, [[characters/arcite\|Arcite]].<br>How do I look? Am I fall’n much away? |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | [[concepts/faith\|Faith]], very little—[[motifs/love\|love]] has used you kindly. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | I’ll warrant thee, I’ll strike [[motifs/home\|home]]. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | Do, and spare not—<br>I’ll give you cause, sweet cousin. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | Now to you, sir.<br>[[characters/palamon\|Palamon]] arms [[characters/arcite\|Arcite]]<br>Methinks this armour’s very like that, [[characters/arcite\|Arcite]],<br>Thou wor’st that day the three kings fell, but lighter. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | That was a very good one, and that day,<br>I [[motifs/well\|well]] remember, you outdid me, cousin.<br>I never saw such valour. When you charged<br>Upon the left wing of the enemy,<br>I spurred hard to come up, and under me<br>I had a right good [[motifs/horse\|horse]]. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | You had indeed—<br>A bright bay, I remember. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | Yes. But all<br>Was vainly laboured in me—you outwent me,<br>Nor could my wishes reach you. Yet a little<br>I did by imitation. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | More by virtue—<br>You are modest, cousin. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | When I saw you charge first,<br>Methought I heard a dreadful clap of thunder<br>Break from the troop. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | But still before that flew<br>The [[motifs/lightning\|lightning]] of your valour. Stay a little,<br>Is not this piece too strait? |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | No, no, ’tis [[motifs/well\|well]]. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | I would have nothing hurt thee but my sword—<br>A bruise would be dishonour. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | Now I am perfect. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | Stand off, then. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | Take my sword; I hold it better. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | I thank ye. No, keep it—your life lies on it.<br>Here’s one—if it but hold, I ask no more<br>For all my hopes. My cause and [[concepts/honour\|honour]] guard me. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | And me, my [[motifs/love\|love]].<br>They bow several ways, then advance and stand<br>Is there aught else to say? |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | This only, and no more. Thou art mine aunt’s son,<br>And that [[motifs/blood\|blood]] we [[motifs/desire\|desire]] to shed is mutual:<br>In me, thine, and in thee, mine. My sword<br>Is in my hand, and if thou kill’st me,<br>The gods and I forgive thee. If there be<br>A place prepared for those that [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]] in [[concepts/honour\|honour]],<br>I wish his weary [[motifs/soul\|soul]] that falls may win it.<br>Fight bravely, cousin. Give me thy noble hand. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | Here, [[characters/palamon\|Palamon]]. This hand shall never more<br>Come near thee with such [[concepts/friendship\|friendship]]. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | I commend thee. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | If I fall, curse me, and say I was a coward—<br>For none but such dare die in these just trials.<br>Once more farewell, my cousin. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | Farewell, [[characters/arcite\|Arcite]].<br>Fight. Horns within; they stand |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | Lo, cousin, lo, our folly has undone us. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | Why? |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | This is the Duke a-hunting, as I told you.<br>If we be found, we are wretched. O, retire,<br>For [[concepts/honour\|honour]]’s sake, and safely, presently,<br>Into your bush again. Sir, we shall find<br>Too many hours to die. In, gentle cousin—<br>If you be seen, you perish instantly<br>For breaking [[motifs/prison\|prison]], and I, if you reveal me,<br>For my contempt. Then all the world will scorn us,<br>And say we had a noble difference,<br>But base disposers of it. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | No, no, cousin,<br>I will no more be hidden, nor put off<br>This great adventure to a second trial.<br>I know your cunning and I know your cause—<br>He that faints now, shame take him! Put thyself<br>Upon thy present guard— |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | You are not mad? |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | Or I will make th’advantage of this hour<br>Mine own, and what to come shall threaten me<br>I [[concepts/fear\|fear]] less than my fortune. Know, weak cousin,<br>I [[motifs/love\|love]] [[characters/emilia\|Emilia]], and in that I’ll bury<br>Thee and all crosses else. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | Then come what can come,<br>Thou shalt know, [[characters/palamon\|Palamon]], I dare as [[motifs/well\|well]]<br>Die as discourse or [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]]. Only this fears me,<br>The law will have the [[concepts/honour\|honour]] of our ends.<br>Have at thy life! |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | Look to thine own [[motifs/well\|well]], [[characters/arcite\|Arcite]]!<br>They fight again. |
| *(didascalia)* | Horns. Enter [[characters/theseus\|Theseus]], [[characters/hippolyta\|Hippolyta]], [[characters/emilia\|Emilia]], Pirithous, and train. [[characters/theseus\|Theseus]] separates [[characters/palamon\|Palamon]] and [[characters/arcite\|Arcite]] |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | What ignorant and mad malicious traitors<br>Are you, that ’gainst the tenor of my laws<br>Are making battle, thus like knights appointed,<br>Without my leave and officers of arms?<br>By Castor, both shall die. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | Hold thy word, [[characters/theseus\|Theseus]].<br>We are certainly both traitors, both despisers<br>Of thee and of thy goodness. I am [[characters/palamon\|Palamon]],<br>That cannot [[motifs/love\|love]] thee, he that broke thy [[motifs/prison\|prison]]—<br>Think [[motifs/well\|well]] what that deserves. And this is [[characters/arcite\|Arcite]];<br>A bolder traitor never trod thy ground,<br>A falser ne’er seemed friend. This is the man<br>Was begged and banished; this is he contemns thee,<br>And what thou dar’st do; and in this disguise,<br>Against thine own edict, follows thy sister,<br>That fortunate bright star, the fair [[characters/emilia\|Emilia]],<br>Whose servant—if there be a right in seeing<br>And first bequeathing of the [[motifs/soul\|soul]] to—justly<br>I am; and, which is more, dares think her his.<br>This treachery, like a most trusty [[archetypes/lover\|lover]],<br>I called him now to answer. If thou be’st<br>As thou art spoken, great and virtuous,<br>The true decider of all injuries,<br>Say, ’Fight again’, and thou shalt see me, [[characters/theseus\|Theseus]],<br>Do such a justice thou thyself wilt envy.<br>Then take my life—I’ll woo thee to’t. |
| PIRITHOUS | O heaven,<br>What more than man is this! |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | I have sworn. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | We seek not<br>Thy breath of [[concepts/mercy\|mercy]], [[characters/theseus\|Theseus]]. ‘Tis to me<br>A thing as soon to die as thee to say it,<br>And no more moved. Where this man calls me traitor<br>Let me say thus much—if in [[motifs/love\|love]] be treason,<br>In service of so excellent a [[concepts/beauty\|beauty]],<br>As I [[motifs/love\|love]] most, and in that [[concepts/faith\|faith]] will perish,<br>As I have brought my life here to confirm it,<br>As I have served her truest, worthiest,<br>As I dare kill this cousin that denies it,<br>So let me be most traitor and ye please me.<br>For scorning thy edict, Duke, ask that lady<br>Why she is fair, and why her eyes command me<br>Stay here to [[motifs/love\|love]] her, and if she say, ‘Traitor’,<br>I am a [[archetypes/villain\|villain]] fit to lie unburied. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | Thou shalt have pity of us both, O [[characters/theseus\|Theseus]],<br>If unto neither thou show [[concepts/mercy\|mercy]]. Stop,<br>As thou art just, thy noble ear against us;<br>As thou art valiant, for thy cousin’s [[motifs/soul\|soul]],<br>Whose twelve strong labours [[motifs/crown\|crown]] his [[motifs/memory\|memory]],<br>Let’s die together, at one instant, Duke.<br>Only a little let him fall before me,<br>That I may tell my [[motifs/soul\|soul]] he shall not have her. |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | I grant your wish; for to say true, your cousin<br>Has ten times more offended, for I gave him<br>More [[concepts/mercy\|mercy]] than you found, sir, your offences<br>Being no more than his. None here speak for ’em,<br>For ere the sun set both shall [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]] for ever. |
| [[characters/hippolyta\|HIPPOLYTA]] | (to [[characters/emilia\|Emilia]])<br>Alas, the pity! Now or never, sister,<br>Speak, not to be denied. That face of yours<br>Will bear the curses else of after ages<br>For these lost cousins. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | In my face, dear sister,<br>I find no anger to ’em, nor no ruin.<br>The misadventure of their own eyes kill ’em.<br>Yet that I will be woman and have pity, |
| *(didascalia)* | She kneels |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | My knees shall grow to th’ ground, but I’ll get [[concepts/mercy\|mercy]].<br>Help me, dear sister—in a deed so virtuous<br>The powers of all women will be with us.<br>[[characters/hippolyta\|Hippolyta]] kneels<br>Most royal brother— |
| [[characters/hippolyta\|HIPPOLYTA]] | Sir, by our tie of [[motifs/marriage\|marriage]]— |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | By your own spotless [[concepts/honour\|honour]]— |
| [[characters/hippolyta\|HIPPOLYTA]] | By that [[concepts/faith\|faith]],<br>That fair hand, and that honest heart you gave me— |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | By that you would have pity in another,<br>By your own virtues infinite— |
| [[characters/hippolyta\|HIPPOLYTA]] | By valour,<br>By all the chaste nights I have ever pleased you— |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | These are strange conjurings. |
| PIRITHOUS | Nay, then, I’ll in too. |
| *(didascalia)* | He kneels |
| PIRITHOUS | By all our [[concepts/friendship\|friendship]], sir, by all our dangers,<br>By all you [[motifs/love\|love]] most: wars, and this sweet lady— |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | By that you would have trembled to deny<br>A blushing maid— |
| [[characters/hippolyta\|HIPPOLYTA]] | By your own eyes, by strength—<br>In which you swore I went beyond all women,<br>Almost all men—and yet I yielded, [[characters/theseus\|Theseus]]— |
| PIRITHOUS | To [[motifs/crown\|crown]] all this, by your most noble [[motifs/soul\|soul]],<br>Which cannot want due [[concepts/mercy\|mercy]], I beg first— |
| [[characters/hippolyta\|HIPPOLYTA]] | Next hear my prayers— |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Last let me entreat, sir— |
| PIRITHOUS | For [[concepts/mercy\|mercy]]. |
| [[characters/hippolyta\|HIPPOLYTA]] | [[concepts/mercy\|Mercy]]. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | [[concepts/mercy\|Mercy]] on these princes. |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | Ye make my [[concepts/faith\|faith]] reel. Say I felt<br>Compassion to ’em both, how would you place it? |
| *(didascalia)* | They rise |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Upon their lives—but with their banishments. |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | You are a right woman, sister: you have pity,<br>But want the understanding where to use it.<br>If you [[motifs/desire\|desire]] their lives, invent a way<br>Safer than banishment. Can these two live,<br>And have the agony of [[motifs/love\|love]] about ‘em,<br>And not kill one another? Every day<br>They’d fight about you, hourly bring your [[concepts/honour\|honour]]<br>In public question with their swords. Be wise, then,<br>And here forget ’em. It concerns your credit<br>And my oath equally. I have said—they die.<br>Better they fall by th’ law than one another.<br>Bow not my [[concepts/honour\|honour]]. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | O my noble brother,<br>That oath was rashly made, and in your anger.<br>Your reason will not hold it. If such vows<br>Stand for express will, all the world must perish.<br>Beside, I have another oath ’gainst yours,<br>Of more authority, I am sure more [[motifs/love\|love]]—<br>Not made in passion, neither, but good heed. |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | What is it, sister? |
| PIRITHOUS | (to [[characters/emilia\|Emilia]]) Urge it [[motifs/home\|home]], brave lady. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | That you would ne’er deny me anything<br>Fit for my modest suit and your free granting.<br>I tie you to your word now; if ye fail in’t,<br>Think how you maim your [[concepts/honour\|honour]]—<br>For now I am set a-begging, sir. I am deaf<br>To all but your compassion—how their lives<br>Might breed the ruin of my name, opinion.<br>Shall anything that loves me perish for me?<br>That were a cruel wisdom: do men prune<br>The straight young boughs that blush with thousand<br>blossoms<br>Because they may be rotten? O, Duke [[characters/theseus\|Theseus]],<br>The goodly mothers that have groaned for these,<br>And all the [[concepts/longing\|longing]] maids that ever loved,<br>If your vow stand, shall curse me and my [[concepts/beauty\|beauty]],<br>And in their funeral songs for these two cousins<br>Despise my cruelty and cry woe worth me,<br>Till I am nothing but the scorn of women.<br>For heaven’s sake, save their lives and banish ’em. |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | On what conditions? |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Swear ’em never more To make me their contention, or to know me,<br>To tread upon thy dukedom; and to be,<br>Wherever they shall travel, ever strangers<br>To one another. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | I’ll be cut a-pieces<br>Before I take this oath—forget I [[motifs/love\|love]] her?<br>O all ye gods, despise me, then. Thy banishment<br>I not mislike, so we may fairly carry<br>Our swords and cause along—else, never trifle,<br>But take our lives, Duke. I must [[motifs/love\|love]], and will;<br>And for that [[motifs/love\|love]] must and dare kill this cousin<br>On any piece the earth has. |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | Will you, [[characters/arcite\|Arcite]],<br>Take these conditions? |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | He’s a [[archetypes/villain\|villain]] then. |
| PIRITHOUS | These are men! |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | No, never, Duke. ’Tis worse to me than begging,<br>To take my life so basely. Though I think<br>I never shall enjoy her, yet I’ll preserve<br>The [[concepts/honour\|honour]] of affection and die for her,<br>Make [[motifs/death\|death]] a devil. |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | What may be done? For now I feel compassion. |
| PIRITHOUS | Let it not fall again, sir. |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | Say, [[characters/emilia\|Emilia]], If one of them were dead—as one must—are you<br>Content to take the other to your husband?<br>They cannot both enjoy you. They are princes<br>As goodly as your own eyes, and as noble<br>As ever fame yet spoke of. Look upon ’em,<br>And if you can [[motifs/love\|love]], end this difference.<br>I give consent. (To [[characters/palamon\|Palamon]] and [[characters/arcite\|Arcite]]) Are you<br>content too, princes? |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | and [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]]<br>With all our souls. |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | He that she refuses<br>Must die, then. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | and [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]]<br>Any [[motifs/death\|death]] thou canst invent, Duke. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | If I fall from that mouth, I fall with favour,<br>And lovers yet unborn shall bless my ashes. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | If she refuse me, yet my [[motifs/grave\|grave]] will wed me,<br>And soldiers sing my epitaph. |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | (to [[characters/emilia\|Emilia]])<br>Make choice, then. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | I cannot, sir. They are both too excellent.<br>For me, a hair shall never fall of these men. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/hippolyta\|HIPPOLYTA]] to [[characters/theseus\|Theseus]] |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | What will become of ’em? |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | Thus I ordain it, And by mine [[concepts/honour\|honour]] once again it stands,<br>Or both shall die. (To [[characters/palamon\|Palamon]] and [[characters/arcite\|Arcite]]) You shall<br>both to your country,<br>And each within this month, accompanied<br>With three fair knights, appear again in this place,<br>In which I’ll plant a pyramid; and whether,<br>Before us that are here, can force his cousin,<br>By fair and knightly strength, to touch the pillar,<br>He shall enjoy her; the other lose his head,<br>And all his friends; nor shall he grudge to fall,<br>Nor think he dies with interest in this lady.<br>Will this content ye? |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | Yes. Here, cousin [[characters/arcite\|Arcite]],<br>I am friends again till that hour. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | I embrace ye. |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | (to [[characters/emilia\|Emilia]])<br>Are you content, sister? |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Yes, I must, sir,<br>Else both miscarry. |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | (to [[characters/palamon\|Palamon]] and [[characters/arcite\|Arcite]])<br>Come, shake hands again, then,<br>And take heed, as you are gentlemen, this quarrel<br>[[motifs/sleep\|Sleep]] till the hour prefixed, and hold your course. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | We dare not fail thee, [[characters/theseus\|Theseus]]. |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | Come, I’ll give ye<br>Now usage like to princes and to friends.<br>When ye return, who wins I’ll settle here,<br>Who loses, yet I’ll weep upon his bier. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt. ln the act-[[motifs/time\|time]] the bush is removed |

### Atto 4, Scena 1

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Jailer and his Friend |
| JAILER | Hear you no more? Was nothing said of me<br>Concerning the escape of [[characters/palamon\|Palamon]]?<br>Good sir, remember. |
| FRIEND | Nothing that I heard,<br>For I came [[motifs/home\|home]] before the business<br>Was fully ended. Yet I might perceive,<br>Ere I departed, a great likelihood<br>Of both their pardons: for [[characters/hippolyta\|Hippolyta]]<br>And fair-eyed Emily upon their knees<br>Begged with such handsome pity that the Duke,<br>Methought, stood staggering whether he should<br>follow<br>His rash oath or the sweet compassion<br>Of those two ladies; and to second them<br>That truly noble prince, Pirithous—<br>Half his own heart—set in too, that I [[concepts/hope\|hope]]<br>All shall be [[motifs/well\|well]]. Neither heard I one question<br>Of your name or his scape. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Second Friend |
| JAILER | Pray heaven it hold so. |
| SECOND FRIEND | Be of good comfort, man. I bring you news,<br>Good news. |
| JAILER | They are welcome. |
| SECOND FRIEND | [[characters/palamon\|Palamon]] has cleared you,<br>And got your pardon, and discovered how<br>And by whose means he scaped—which was your<br>daughter’s,<br>Whose pardon is procured too; and the prisoner,<br>Not to be held ungrateful to her goodness,<br>Has given a sum of money to her [[motifs/marriage\|marriage]]—<br>A large one, I’ll assure you. |
| JAILER | Ye are a good man,<br>And ever bring good news. |
| FIRST FRIEND | How was it ended? |
| SECOND FRIEND | Why, as it should be: they that ne’er begged,<br>But they prevailed, had their suits fairly granted—<br>The prisoners have their lives. |
| FIRST FRIEND | I knew ’twould be so. |
| SECOND FRIEND | But there be new conditions which you’ll hear of<br>At better [[motifs/time\|time]]. |
| JAILER | I [[concepts/hope\|hope]] they are good. |
| SECOND FRIEND | They are honourable—<br>How good they’ll prove I know not. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Wooer |
| EIRST ERIEND | ’Twill be known. |
| WOOER | Alas, sir, where’s your daughter? |
| JAILER | Why do you ask? |
| WOOER | O, sir, when did you see her? |
| SECOND FRIEND | How he looks! |
| JAILER | This morning. |
| WOOER | Was she [[motifs/well\|well]]? Was she in health?<br>Sir, when did she [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]]? |
| FIRST FRIEND | These are strange questions. |
| JAILER | I do not think she was very [[motifs/well\|well]]: for now<br>You make me mind her, but this very day<br>I asked her questions and she answered me<br>So far from what she was, so childishly,<br>So sillily, as if she were a [[archetypes/fool\|fool]],<br>An [[archetypes/innocent\|innocent]]—and I was very angry.<br>But what of her, sir? |
| WOOER | Nothing, but my pity<br>But you must know it, and as good by me<br>As by another that less loves her— |
| JAILER | [[motifs/well\|Well]], sir? |
| FIRST FRIEND | Not right? |
| WOOER | No, sir, not [[motifs/well\|well]]. |
| SECOND FRIEND | Not [[motifs/well\|Well]]? |
| WOOER | ’Tis too true—she is mad. |
| FIRST FRIEND | It cannot be. |
| WOOER | Believe, you’ll find it so. |
| JAILER | I half suspected<br>What you told me—the gods comfort her!<br>Either this was her [[motifs/love\|love]] to [[characters/palamon\|Palamon]],<br>Or [[concepts/fear\|fear]] of my miscarrying on his scape,<br>Or both. |
| WOOER | ’Tis likely. |
| JAILER | But why all this haste, sir? |
| WOOER | I’ll tell you quickly. As I late was angling<br>In the great lake that lies behind the palace,<br>From the far shore, thick set with reeds and sedges,<br>As patiently I was attending sport,<br>I heard a voice—a shrill one—and attentive<br>I gave my ear, when I might [[motifs/well\|well]] perceive<br>’Twas one that sung, and by the smallness of it<br>A boy or woman. I then left my angle<br>To his own skill, came near, but yet perceived not<br>Who made the sound, the rushes and the reeds<br>Had so encompassed it. I laid me down<br>And listened to the words she sung, for then,<br>Through a small glade cut by the fishermen,<br>I saw it was your daughter. |
| JAILER | Pray go on, sir. |
| WOOER | She sung much, but no sense; only I heard her<br>Repeat this often—‘[[characters/palamon\|Palamon]] is gone,<br>Is gone to th’ wood to gather mulberries;<br>I’ll find him out tomorrow.’ |
| FIRST FRIEND | Pretty [[motifs/soul\|soul]]! |
| WOOER | ‘His shackles will betray him—he’ll be taken,<br>And what shall I do then? I’ll bring a bevy,<br>A hundred black-eyed maids that [[motifs/love\|love]] as I do,<br>With chaplets on their heads of daffodillies,<br>With cherry lips and cheeks of damask roses,<br>And all we’ll [[motifs/dance\|dance]] an antic fore the Duke<br>And beg his pardon.’ Then she talked of you, sir—<br>That you must lose your head tomorrow morning,<br>And she must gather [[motifs/flowers\|flowers]] to bury you,<br>And see the house made handsome. Then she sung<br>Nothing but ‘willow, willow, willow’, and between<br>Ever was ‘[[characters/palamon\|Palamon]], fair [[characters/palamon\|Palamon]]’,<br>And ‘[[characters/palamon\|Palamon]] was a tall young man’. The place<br>Was knee-deep where she sat; her careless tresses<br>A wreath of bull-rush rounded; about her stuck<br>Thousand freshwater [[motifs/flowers\|flowers]] of several colours—<br>That she appeared, methought, like the fair nymph<br>That feeds the lake with waters, or as Iris<br>Newly dropped down from heaven. Rings she made<br>Of rushes that grew by, and to ‘em spoke<br>The prettiest posies—‘Thus our true [[motifs/love\|love]]’s tied’,<br>‘This you may lose, not me’, and many a one.<br>And then she wept, and sung again, and sighed—<br>And with the same breath smiled and kissed her<br>hand. |
| SECOND FRIEND | Alas, what pity it is! |
| WOOER | I made in to her:<br>She saw me and straight sought the flood—I saved<br>her,<br>And set her safe to land, when presently<br>She slipped away and to the [[motifs/city\|city]] made,<br>With such a cry and swiftness that, believe me,<br>She left me far behind her. Three or four<br>I saw from far off [[motifs/cross\|cross]] her—one of ’em<br>I knew to be your brother, where she stayed<br>And fell, scarce to be got away. I left them with her, |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Jailer’s Brother, the Jailer’s Daughter, and others |
| WOOER | And hither came to tell you—here they are. |
| JAILER’S DAUGHTER | (sings)<br>‘May you never more enjoy the light ...’—<br>Is not this a fine song? |
| JAILER’S BROTHER | O, a very fine one. |
| JAILER’S DAUGHTER | I can sing twenty more. |
| JAILER’S BROTHER | I think you can. |
| JAILER’S DAUGHTER | Yes, truly can I—I can sing ‘The Broom’<br>And ‘Bonny Robin’—are not you a tailor? |
| JAILER’S BROTHER | Yes. |
| JAILER’S DAUGHTER | Where’s my wedding gown?<br>JAILER’s BROTHER<br>I’ll bring it tomorrow. |
| JAILER’S DAUGHTER | Do, very rarely—I must be abroad else,<br>To call the maids and pay the minstrels,<br>For I must lose my maidenhead by cocklight,<br>‘Twill never thrive else. (Sings) ‘O fair, O sweet ...’ |
| *(didascalia)* | JAILER’S BROTHER to the jailer |
| JAILER’S DAUGHTER | You must e’en take it patiently. |
| JAILER | ’Tis true. |
| JAILER’S DAUGHTER | Good ev’n, good men. Pray, did you ever hear<br>Of one young [[characters/palamon\|Palamon]]? |
| JAILER | Yes, wench, we know him. |
| JAILER’S DAUGHTER | Is’t not a fine young gentleman? |
| JAILER | ’Tis, [[motifs/love\|love]]. |
| JAILER’S BROTHER | By no mean [[motifs/cross\|cross]] her, she is then distempered<br>Far worse than now she shows. |
| FIRST FRIEND | (to the Jailer’s Daughter)<br>Yes, he’s a fine man. |
| JAILER’S DAUGHTER | O, is he so? You have a sister. |
| FIRST FRIEND | Yes. |
| JAILER’S DAUGHTER | But she shall never have him, tell her so,<br>For a trick that I know. You’d best look to her,<br>For if she see him once, she’s gone—she’s done<br>And undone in an hour. All the young maids<br>Of our town are in [[motifs/love\|love]] with him, but I laugh at<br>’em<br>And let ’em all alone. Is’t not a wise course? |
| FIRST FRIEND | Yes. |
| JAILER’S DAUGHTER | There is at least two hundred now with [[archetypes/child\|child]] by him,<br>There must be four; yet I keep close for all this,<br>Close as a cockle; and all these must be boys—<br>He has the trick on’t—and at ten years old<br>They must be all gelt for musicians<br>And sing the wars of [[characters/theseus\|Theseus]]. |
| SECOND FRIEND | This is strange. |
| *(didascalia)* | JAILER’S BROTHER |
| SECOND FRIEND | As ever you heard, but say nothing. |
| FIRST FRIEND | No. |
| JAILER’S DAUGHTER | They come from all parts of the dukedom to him.<br>I’ll warrant ye, he had not so few last [[motifs/night\|night]]<br>As twenty to dispatch. He’ll tickle’t up<br>In two hours, if his hand be in. |
| JAILER | She’s lost<br>Past all cure. |
| JAILER’S BROTHER | Heaven forbid, man! |
| JAILER’S DAUGHTER | (to the Jailer)<br>Come hither—you are a wise man. |
| FIRST FRIEND | Does she know him? |
| SECOND FRIEND | No—would she did. |
| JAILER’S DAUGHTER | You are master of a [[motifs/ship\|ship]]? |
| JAILER | Yes. |
| JAILER’S DAUGHTER | Where’s your compass? |
| JAILER | Here. |
| JAILER’S DAUGHTER | Set it to th’ north.<br>And now direct your course to th’ wood where<br>[[characters/palamon\|Palamon]]<br>Lies [[concepts/longing\|longing]] for me. For the tackling,<br>Let me alone. Come, weigh, my hearts, cheerly all.<br>Uff, uff, uff! ’Tis up. The [[motifs/wind\|wind]]’s fair. Top the bowline.<br>Out with the mainsail. Where’s your whistle, master? |
| JAILER’S BROTHER | Let’s get her in. |
| JAILER | Up to the top, boy! |
| JAILER’S BROTHER | Where’s the pilot? |
| FIRST FRIEND | Here. |
| JAILER’S DAUGHTER | What kenn’st thou? |
| SECOND FRIEND | A fair wood. |
| JAILER’S DAUGHTER | Bear for it, master.<br>Tack about!<br>(Sings) ‘When [[characters/cynthia\|Cynthia]] with her borrowed light ...’ |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |
| *(didascalia)* | 4.2 Enter [[characters/emilia\|Emilia]], with two pictures |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Yet I may bind those wounds up that must open<br>And bleed to [[motifs/death\|death]] for my sake else—I’ll choose,<br>And end their strife. Two such young handsome men<br>Shall never fall for me; their weeping mothers<br>Following the dead cold ashes of their sons,<br>Shall never curse my cruelty. Good heaven,<br>What a sweet face has Arcitel If wise [[concepts/nature\|nature]],<br>With all her best endowments, all those beauties<br>She sows into the births of noble bodies,<br>Were here a mortal woman and had in her<br>The coy denials of young maids, yet doubtless<br>She would run mad for this man. What an eye,<br>Of what a fiery sparkle and quick sweetness<br>Has this young prince! Here [[motifs/love\|love]] himself sits smiling!<br>Just such another wanton Ganymede<br>Set Jove afire once, and enforced the god<br>Snatch up the goodly boy and set him by him,<br>A shining constellation. What a brow,<br>Of what a spacious majesty, he carries!<br>Arched like the great-eyed Juno’s, but far sweeter,<br>Smoother than Pelops’ shoulder! Fame and [[concepts/honour\|honour]],<br>Methinks, from hence, as from a promontory<br>Pointed in heaven, should clap their wings and sing<br>To all the under world the loves and fights<br>Of gods, and such men near ’em. [[characters/palamon\|Palamon]]<br>Is but his foil; to him a mere dull [[archetypes/shadow\|shadow]];<br>He’s swart and meagre, of an eye as heavy<br>As if he had lost his mother; a still temper,<br>No stirring in him, no alacrity,<br>Of all this sprightly sharpness, not a smile.<br>Yet these that we count errors may become him:<br>Narcissus was a sad boy, but a heavenly.<br>O, who can find the bent of woman’s fancy?<br>I am a [[archetypes/fool\|fool]], my reason is lost in me,<br>I have no choice, and I have lied so lewdly<br>That women ought to beat me. On my knees<br>I ask thy pardon, [[characters/palamon\|Palamon]], thou art alone<br>And only beautiful, and these the eyes,<br>These the bright lamps of [[concepts/beauty\|beauty]], that command<br>And threaten [[motifs/love\|love]]—and what young maid dare [[motifs/cross\|cross]]<br>’em?<br>What a bold gravity, and yet inviting,<br>Has this brown manly face? O, [[motifs/love\|love]], this only<br>From this hour is complexion. Lie there, [[characters/arcite\|Arcite]],<br>Thou art a changeling to him, a mere gypsy,<br>And this the noble [[concepts/body\|body]]. I am sotted,<br>Utterly lost—my virgin’s [[concepts/faith\|faith]] has fled me.<br>For if my brother, but even now, had asked me<br>Whether I loved, I had run mad for [[characters/arcite\|Arcite]];<br>Now if my sister, more for [[characters/palamon\|Palamon]].<br>Stand both together. Now come ask me, brother—<br>Alas, I know not; ask me now, sweet sister—<br>I may go look. What a mere [[archetypes/child\|child]] is fancy,<br>That having two fair gauds of equal sweetness,<br>Cannot distinguish, but must cry for both! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Gentlemanl |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | How now, sir? |
| GENTLEMAN | From the noble Duke your brother,<br>Madam, I bring you news. The knights are come. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | To end the quarrel? |
| GENTLEMAN | Yes. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Would I might end first!<br>What sins have I committed, chaste [[characters/diana\|Diana]],<br>That my unspotted youth must now be soiled<br>With [[motifs/blood\|blood]] of princes, and my chastity<br>Be made the altar where the lives of lovers—<br>Two greater and two better never yet<br>Made mothers joy—must be the sacrifice<br>To my unhappy [[concepts/beauty\|beauty]]? |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/theseus\|Theseus]], [[characters/hippolyta\|Hippolyta]], Pirithous, and attendants |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | Bring ’em in<br>Quickly, by any means, I long to see ’em. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit one or more |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | (To [[characters/emilia\|Emilia]]) Your two contending lovers are returned,<br>And with them their fair knights. Now, my fair sister,<br>You must [[motifs/love\|love]] one of them. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | I had rather both,<br>So neither for my sake should fall untimely. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Messenger |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | Who saw ’em? |
| PIRITHOUS | I a while. |
| GENTLEMAN | And I. |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | (to the Messenger)<br>From whence come you, sir? |
| MESSENGER | From the knights. |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | Pray speak,<br>You that have seen them, what they are. |
| MESSENGER | I will, sir,<br>And truly what I think. Six braver spirits<br>Than these they have brought, if we judge by the<br>outside,<br>I never saw nor read of. He that stands<br>In the first place with [[characters/arcite\|Arcite]], by his seeming,<br>Should be a stout man; by his face, a prince.<br>His very looks so say him: his complexion,<br>Nearer a brown than black, stern and yet noble,<br>Which shows him hardy, fearless, proud of dangers.<br>The circles of his eyes show [[motifs/fire\|fire]] within him,<br>And, as a heated [[motifs/lion\|lion]], so he looks.<br>His hair hangs long behind him, black and shining,<br>Like ravens’ wings. His shoulders, broad and strong;<br>Armed long and round; and on his thigh a sword<br>Hung by a curious baldric, when he frowns<br>To seal his will with. Better, o’ my [[concepts/conscience\|conscience]],<br>Was never soldier’s friend. |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | Thou hast [[motifs/well\|well]] described him. |
| PIRITHOUS | Yet a great deal short,<br>Methinks, of him that’s first with [[characters/palamon\|Palamon]]. |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | Pray speak him, friend. |
| PIRITHOUS | I guess he is a prince too,<br>And, if it may be, greater—for his show<br>Has all the ornament of [[concepts/honour\|honour]] in’t.<br>He’s somewhat bigger than the knight he spoke of,<br>But of a face far sweeter. His complexion<br>Is as a ripe grape, ruddy. He has felt,<br>Without [[concepts/doubt\|doubt]], what he fights for, and so apter<br>To make this cause his own. In’s face appears<br>All the fair hopes of what he undertakes,<br>And when he’s angry, then a settled valour,<br>Not tainted with extremes, runs through his [[concepts/body\|body]]<br>And guides his arm to brave things. [[concepts/fear\|Fear]] he cannot—<br>He shows no such soft temper. His head’s yellow,<br>Hard-haired and curled, thick twined: like ivy tods,<br>Not to undo with thunder. In his face<br>The livery of the warlike maid appears,<br>Pure red and white—for yet no beard has blessed<br>him—<br>And in his rolling eyes sits victory,<br>As if she ever meant to [[settings/court\|court]] his valour.<br>His nose stands high, a character of [[concepts/honour\|honour]];<br>His red lips, after fights, are fit for ladies. |
| [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] | Must these men die too? |
| PIRITHOUS | When he speaks, his tongue |
| *(didascalia)* | Sounds like a trumpet. All his lineaments |
| PIRITHOUS | Are as a man would wish ’em—strong and clean.<br>He wears a [[motifs/well\|well]]-steeled axe, the staff of gold.<br>His age, some five-and-twenty. |
| MESSENGER | There’s another—<br>A little man, but of a tough [[motifs/soul\|soul]], seeming<br>As great as any. Fairer promises<br>In such a [[concepts/body\|body]] yet I never looked on. |
| PIRITHOUS | O, he that’s freckle-faced? |
| MESSENGER | The same, my lord.<br>Are they not sweet ones? |
| PIRITHOUS | Yes, they are [[motifs/well\|well]]. |
| MESSENGER | Methinks,<br>Being so few and [[motifs/well\|well]] disposed, they show<br>Great and fine art in [[concepts/nature\|nature]]. He’s white-haired—<br>Not wanton white, but such a manly colour<br>Next to an auburn, tough and nimble set,<br>Which shows an active [[motifs/soul\|soul]]. His arms are brawny,<br>Lined with strong sinews—to the shoulder piece<br>Gently they swell, like women new-conceived,<br>Which speaks him prone to labour, never fainting<br>Under the weight of arms; stout-hearted, still,<br>But when he stirs, a tiger. He’s grey-eyed,<br>Which yields compassion where he conquers; sharp<br>To spy advantages, and where he finds ’em,<br>He’s swift to make ’em his. He does no wrongs,<br>Nor takes none. He’s round-faced, and when he smiles<br>He shows a [[archetypes/lover\|lover]]; when he frowns, a soldier.<br>About his head he wears the winner’s oak,<br>And in it stuck the favour of his lady.<br>His age, some six-and-thirty. In his hand<br>He bears a charging staff embossed with silver. |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | Are they all thus? |
| PIRITHOUS | They are all the sons of [[concepts/honour\|honour]]. |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | Now as I have a [[motifs/soul\|soul]], I long to see ’em.<br>(To [[characters/hippolyta\|Hippolyta]]) Lady, you shall see men fight now. |
| [[characters/hippolyta\|HIPPOLYTA]] | I Wish it,<br>But not the cause, my lord. They would show<br>Bravely about the titles of two kingdoms—<br>’Tis pity [[motifs/love\|love]] should be so tyrannous.<br>(To [[characters/emilia\|Emilia]]) O my soft-hearted sister, what think you?<br>Weep not till they weep [[motifs/blood\|blood]]. Wench, it must be. |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | (to [[characters/emilia\|Emilia]])<br>You have steeled ’em with your [[concepts/beauty\|beauty]].<br>(To Pirithous)<br>Honoured friend, To you I give the field: pray order it<br>Fitting the persons that must use it. |
| PIRITHOUS | Yes, sir. |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | Come, I’ll go visit ’em—I cannot stay,<br>Their fame has fired me so. Till they appear,<br>Good friend, be royal. |
| PIRITHOUS | There shall want no bravery. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/emilia\|EMILIA]] aside |
| PIRITHOUS | Poor wench, go weep—for whosoever wins<br>Loses a noble cousin for thy sins. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 4, Scena 3

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Jailer, the Wooer, and the Doctor |
| DOCTOR | Her distraction is more at some [[motifs/time\|time]] of the [[motifs/moon\|moon]] than at other some, is it not? |
| JAILER | She is continually in a harmless distemper: sleeps little; altogether without appetite, save often drinking; dreaming of another world, and a better; and what broken piece of matter soe’er she’s about, the name ’[[characters/palamon\|Palamon]]’ lards it, that she farces every business |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Jailer’s Daughter |
| JAILER | withal, fits it to every question. Look where she comes—you shall perceive her behaviour.<br>They stand apart |
| JAILER’S DAUGHTER | I have forgot it quite—the burden on’t was ‘Down-a, down-a’, and penned by no worse man than Giraldo, [[characters/emilia\|Emilia]]’s schoolmaster. He’s as fantastical, too, as ever he may go upon’s legs—for in the next world will Dido see [[characters/palamon\|Palamon]], and then will she be out of [[motifs/love\|love]] with Aeneas. |
| DOCTOR | What stuff’s here? Poor [[motifs/soul\|soul]]. |
| JAILER | E’en thus all day long. |
| JAILER’S DAUGHTER | Now for this charm that I told you of—you must bring a piece of silver on the tip of your tongue, or no ferry: then, if it be your chance to come where the blessed spirits are—there’s a sight now! We maids that have our livers perished, cracked to pieces with [[motifs/love\|love]], we shall come there and do nothing all day long but pick [[motifs/flowers\|flowers]] with Proserpine. Then will I make [[characters/palamon\|Palamon]] a nosegay, then let him mark me, then— |
| DOCTOR | How prettily she’s amiss! Note her a little further. |
| JAILER’S DAUGHTER | [[concepts/faith\|Faith]], I’ll tell you: sometime we go to barley-break, we of the blessed. Alas, ’tis a sore life they have i’th’ other place—such burning, frying, boiling, hissing, howling, chattering, cursing—O they have shrewd measure—take heed! If one be mad or hang or drown themselves, thither they go, Jupiter bless us, and there shall we be put in a cauldron of lead and usurers’ grease, amongst a whole million of cutpurses, and there boil like a gammon of bacon that will never be enough. |
| DOCTOR | How her brain coins! |
| JAILER’S DAUGHTER | Lords and courtiers that have got maids with [[archetypes/child\|child]]—they are in this place. They shall stand in [[motifs/fire\|fire]] up to the navel and in ice up to th’ heart, and there th’offending part burns, and the deceiving part freezes—in [[concepts/truth\|truth]] a very grievous punishment as one would think for such a trifle. Believe me, one would marry a leprous [[characters/witch\|witch]] to be rid on’t, I’ll assure you. |
| DOCTOR | How she continues this fancy! ’Tis not an engrafted [[concepts/madness\|madness]], but a most thick and profound [[concepts/melancholy\|melancholy]]. |
| JAILER’S DAUGHTER | To hear there a proud lady and a proud [[motifs/city\|city]] wife howl together! I were a beast an I’d call it good sport. One cries, ‘O this smoke!’, th‘other, ‘This [[motifs/fire\|fire]]!’; one cries, ‘O that ever I did it behind the arras!’, and then howls—th’other curses a suing fellow and her [[motifs/garden\|garden]]-house.<br>(Sings) ‘I will be true, my [[motifs/stars\|stars]], my fate . . .’ |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Daughter |
| JAILER | (to the Doctor) What think you of her, sir? |
| DOCTOR | I think she has a perturbed mind, which I cannot minister to. |
| JAILER | Alas, what then? |
| DOCTOR | Understand you she ever affected any man ere she beheld [[characters/palamon\|Palamon]]? |
| JAILER | I was once, sir, in great [[concepts/hope\|hope]] she had fixed her liking on this gentleman, my friend. |
| WOOER | I did think so too, and would account I had a great penn’orth on’t to give half my state that both she and I, at this present, stood unfeignedly on the same terms. 66 |
| DOCTOR | That intemperate surfeit of her eye hath distempered the other senses. They may return and settle again to execute their preordained faculties, but they are now in a most extravagant vagary. This you must do: confine her to a place where the light may rather seem to steal in than be permitted; take upon you, young sir her friend, the name of [[characters/palamon\|Palamon]]; say you come to eat with her and to commune of [[motifs/love\|love]]. This will catch her attention, for this her mind beats upon—other objects that are inserted ’tween her mind and eye become the pranks and friskins of her [[concepts/madness\|madness]]. Sing to her such green songs of [[motifs/love\|love]] as she says [[characters/palamon\|Palamon]] hath sung in [[motifs/prison\|prison]]; come to her stuck in as sweet [[motifs/flowers\|flowers]] as the season is mistress of, and thereto make an addition of some other compounded odours which are grateful to the sense. All this shall become [[characters/palamon\|Palamon]], for [[characters/palamon\|Palamon]] can sing, and [[characters/palamon\|Palamon]] is sweet and every good thing. [[motifs/desire\|Desire]] to eat with her, carve her, drink to her, and still among intermingle your petition of grace and acceptance into her favour. Learn what maids have been her companions and playferes, and let them repair to her, with [[characters/palamon\|Palamon]] in their mouths, and appear with tokens as if they suggested for him. It is a falsehood she is in, which is with falsehoods to be combated. This may bring her to eat, to [[motifs/sleep\|sleep]], and reduce what’s now out of square in her into their former law and regiment. I have seen it approved, how many times I know not, but to make the number more I have great [[concepts/hope\|hope]] in this. I will between the passages of this project come in with my appliance. Let us put it in execution, and hasten the success, which [[concepts/doubt\|doubt]] not will bring forth comfort. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |
| *(didascalia)* | 5.1 An altar prepared. Flourish. Enter [[characters/theseus\|Theseus]], Pirithous, [[characters/hippolyta\|Hippolyta]], attendants |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | Now let ’em enter and before the gods<br>Tender their holy prayers. Let the temples<br>Burn bright with sacred fires, and the altars<br>In hallowed clouds commend their swelling incense<br>To those above us. Let no due be wanting. |
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish of cornetts |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | They have a noble work in hand, will [[concepts/honour\|honour]]<br>The very powers that [[motifs/love\|love]] ’em. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[characters/palamon\|Palamon]] with his three Knights at one door, and [[characters/arcite\|Arcite]] with his three Knights at the other door |
| PIRITHOUS | Sir, they enter. |
| [[characters/theseus\|THESEUS]] | You valiant and strong-hearted enemies,<br>You royal german foes that this day come<br>To blow that nearness out that flames between ye,<br>Lay by your anger for an hour and, dove-like,<br>Before the holy altars of your helpers,<br>The all-feared gods, bow down your stubborn bodies.<br>Your ire is more than mortal—so your help be;<br>And as the gods regard ye, fight with justice.<br>I’ll leave you to your prayers, and betwixt ye<br>I part my wishes. |
| PIRITHOUS | [[concepts/honour\|Honour]] [[motifs/crown\|crown]] the worthiest. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[characters/theseus\|Theseus]] and his train |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | (to [[characters/arcite\|Arcite]])<br>The glass is running now that cannot finish<br>Till one of us expire. Think you but thus,<br>That were there aught in me which strove to show<br>Mine enemy in this business, were’t one eye<br>Against another, arm oppressed by arm,<br>I would destroy th’offender—coz, I would,<br>Though parcel of myself. Then from this gather<br>How I should tender you. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | I am in labour<br>To push your name, your ancient [[motifs/love\|love]], our kindred,<br>Out of my [[motifs/memory\|memory]], and i’th’ selfsame place<br>To seat something I would confound. So hoist we<br>The sails that must these vessels port even where<br>The heavenly limiter pleases. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | You speak [[motifs/well\|well]].<br>Before I turn, let me embrace thee, cousin—<br>This I shall never do again. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | One farewell. |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | Why, let it be so—farewell, coz. |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | Farewell, sir. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[characters/palamon\|Palamon]] and his three Knights |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | Knights, kinsmen, lovers—yea, my sacrifices,<br>True worshippers of Mars, whose spirit in you<br>Expels the seeds of [[concepts/fear\|fear]] and th’apprehension<br>Which still is father of it, go with me<br>Before the god of our profession. There<br>Require of him the hearts of lions and<br>The breath of tigers, yea, the fierceness too,<br>Yea, the [[characters/speed\|speed]] also—to go on, I mean,<br>Else wish we to be snails. You know my prize<br>Must be dragged out of [[motifs/blood\|blood]]—force and great feat<br>Must put my garland on me, where she sticks,<br>The [[characters/queen\|queen]] of [[motifs/flowers\|flowers]]. Our intercession, then,<br>Must be to him that makes the camp a cistern<br>Brimmed with the [[motifs/blood\|blood]] of men—give me your aid,<br>And bend your spirits towards him. |
| *(didascalia)* | They kneel before the altar, fall on their faces, then on their knees again |
| [[characters/arcite\|ARCITE]] | (Praying to Mars)<br>Thou mighty one,<br>That with thy power hast turned green Neptune into<br>purple;<br>Whose havoc in vast field comets prewarn,<br>Unearthed skulls proclaim; whose breath blows down<br>The teeming Ceres’ foison; who dost pluck<br>With hand armipotent from forth blue clouds<br>The masoned turrets, that both mak’st and break’st<br>The stony girths of cities; me thy pupil,<br>Youngest follower of thy drum, instruct this day<br>With military skill, that to thy laud<br>I may advance my streamer, and by thee<br>Be styled the lord o’th’ day. Give me, great Mars,<br>Some token of thy pleasure.<br>Here they fall on their faces, as formerly, and there is heard clanging of armour, with a short thunder, as the burst of a battle, whereupon they all rise and bow to the altar<br>O great corrector of enormous times,<br>Shaker of o’er-rank states, thou grand decider<br>Of dusty and old titles, that heal’st with [[motifs/blood\|blood]]<br>The earth when it is sick, and cur’st the world<br>O’th’ plurisy of people, I do take<br>Thy signs auspiciously, and in thy name,<br>To my design, march boldly. (To his Knights) Let us go. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 5, Scena 2

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter |
| *(didascalia)* | [[characters/palamon\|Palamon]] and his Knights with the former observance |
| [[characters/palamon\|PALAMON]] | (to his Knights)<br>Our [[motifs/stars\|stars]] must glister with new [[motifs/fire\|fire]], or be<br>Today extinct. Our argument is [[motifs/love\|love]],<br>Which if the goddess of it grant, she gives<br>Victory too. Then blend your spirits with mine,<br>You whose free nobleness do make my cause<br>Your personal hazard. To the goddess [[characters/venus\|Venus]]<br>Commend we our proceeding, and implore<br>Her power unto our party.<br>Here they kneel before the altar,<br>fall on’their faces then on their knees again<br>(Praying to [[characters/venus\|Venus]]) Hail, sovereign [[characters/queen\|queen]] of [[motifs/secrets\|secrets]],<br>who hast power<br>To call the fiercest [[archetypes/tyrant\|tyrant]] from his rage<br>And weep unto a girl; that hast the might,<br>Even with an eye-glance, to choke Mars’s drum<br>And turn th‘alarum to whispers; that canst make<br>A cripple flourish with his crutch, and cure him<br>Before [[characters/apollo\|Apollo]]; that mayst force the [[archetypes/king\|king]]<br>To be his subject’s vassal, and induce<br>Stale gravity to [[motifs/dance\|dance]]; the polled bachelor<br>Whose youth, like wanton boys through bonfires,<br>Have skipped thy flame, at seventy thou canst catch<br>And make him to the scorn of his hoarse throat<br>Abuse young lays of [[motifs/love\|love]]. What godlike power<br>Hast thou not power upon? To Phoebus thou<br>Add’st flames hotter than his—the heavenly fires<br>Did scorch his mortal son, thine him. The huntress,<br>All moist and cold, some say, began to throw<br>Her bow away and sigh. Take to thy grace<br>Me, thy vowed soldier, who do bear thy yoke<br>As ’twere a wreath of roses, yet is heavier<br>Than lead itself, stings more than nettles.<br>I have never been foul-mouthed against thy law;<br>Ne’er revealed secret, for I knew none; would not,<br>Had I kenned all that were. I never practised<br>Upon man’s wife, nor would the libels read<br>Of liberal wits. I never at great feasts<br>Sought to betray a [[concepts/beauty\|beauty]], but have blushed<br>At simp’ring sirs that did.
